To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

B565th of 24

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: there is no formal guidance for FY2026 or FY2027; the only formal guidance is the 2028 target set, and on that horizon management's targets sit above consensus on revenue (consensus SEK 6.88bn is about 4% below the 18% floor of about SEK 7.1bn) and above consensus on absolute EBIT (consensus SEK 363m against a three-to-four-fold ambition from 2024 of about SEK 400 to 540m), while consensus's 5.3% margin sits within the 4.0 to 7.0% range. For FY2026 and FY2027 the company's qualitative commentary (Growth investment elevated through mid-2027; lower-funnel marketing cut from Q4 2026) is broadly consistent with consensus.

Why the gap exists:

  • Management sets long-dated targets and avoids annual guidance to keep flexibility over marketing; brokers, burned by profit misses in 2025 and 2026, model the Growth segment's losses for longer (Deutsche Bank cut its 2027 Growth EBITDA margin to -4.5% from -1.2% after Q3 2026).
  • Thin coverage: consensus is two or three brokers; Pareto (Hold) is well below the others on 2026 and 2027 EPS (0.84 and 3.58 against 2.19 to 2.91 and 5.80 to 5.89).
  • The company publishes a Modular Finance consensus compilation before each report (Q3 2026: revenue SEK 1,212m, adjusted EBIT SEK 30m, EPS SEK 0.40), which is the market's working benchmark for quarterly beats and misses.
C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

F4421st of 24

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: the revenue target is not conservative: because 2025 was a lost year, even the 18% floor needs about 22% growth in each of 2027 and 2028 from about SEK 4.8bn in 2026, above the 2021 to 2025 CAGR of about 14%, at a time when the ZYN base effect ends. The stronger input forces (US online migration, FDA approvals, state taxes) make the floor achievable but the midpoint (about 29% a year) and top (about 37%) unlikely. The margin target (5.5% plus or minus 1.5 points) is above any year in the company's history (adjusted EBIT peak 3.9% in 2025) but is supported by Core's demonstrated maturity margins; its low end (4%) is realistic, its midpoint demanding.

Reasoning:

  • History: revenue CAGR about 14% (2021 to 2025); adjusted EBIT margin 2.5% (2023), 3.7% (2024), 3.9% (2025), about 2.2% (2026E).
  • Input forces versus the past: stronger now (US online penetration rising from 3%, Growth volume growth 60 to 90%, compliant-player consolidation) than in 2023 to 2025 (ZYN shortage, licence dispute, California exit). That justifies growth above the historical CAGR, but not at 29 to 37% a year for two years on a group basis when Core grows high single digits.
  • Margin path required: from about 2.2% (2026) to 5.5% (2028) requires about SEK 300m of extra EBIT on about SEK 2.2 to 3bn of extra revenue, an incremental margin of 10 to 14%, consistent with the contribution margin only if marketing falls and G&A grows slowly.
  • Verdict for the model: lean 2028 revenue to about SEK 6.6 to 7.0bn (at or just below the floor) and the 2028 adjusted EBIT margin to about 4.5 to 5.0%.

6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement44C-7958D-0250D-0336D-0436D-0558D-0650
Group judgement

Risks

–44

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecast is a sound central case whose revenue and whose order of events are predictable and whose earnings per share are not. I would describe the case to Richard as simple in structure and fragile in numbers.

Simple, because three quantities decide it and all three are reported every quarter: the number of Growth customers, supplier-paid income as a share of sales, and operating costs. The earlier sets support each other on this structure (D-01); the revenue and gross-profit side needs only what the business has repeatedly done (D-02); the trace from those quantities to adjusted EBITDA is short and checkable (D-06); and the share price answers to the company alone (D-07).

Fragile, for five reasons that compound. The margin is thin, so small errors in kronor are large errors in earnings (D-04). The profit increase depends on management not spending its gross profit growth, which it did not manage in 2026 and which the open window in the first half of 2027 will test again (D-02). The growth in gross profit is supplier-paid and answers to three manufacturers (D-03). Between EBIT and earnings per share sit four items nobody can forecast, and the valuation rests on the most favourable of three earnings definitions (D-06). And the 18-month return at the guideline multiple is the difference between a 41% fall in the multiple and a 65% rise in forward earnings that depends on 2029 and 2030 (D-04).

What makes it more predictable than that list suggests: Core alone earns about SEK 364m of EBITDA against SEK 220m for the group; marketing and price reinvestment (about SEK 115m together in 2027) can be withdrawn within a quarter or two; the gross-margin input holds a cushion of about SEK 50m against slower supplier income; the 2027 supplier contracts are fixed early in the year and visible by May; and the calendar of tests is known.

Where I think differently from the earlier sets, in sum: the forecast is a forecast of cost behaviour rather than of marketing alone or overhead alone; the United States customer is not worth less than the Nordic one, so the Growth margin is a question of scale against overhead; Core's margin shares the group's dependence on supplier income; the Media and Insights sensitivity is smaller than the second set says and the tax rate lower in 2026 and after 2028; and the next two reports cannot confirm the thesis.

For the memo's view of where the share is in its cycle: the business is turning up but unconfirmed. The margin trough is in the second half of 2026; the median broker has not reset 2027; the first confirming evidence is due in mid-August 2027. What would raise my assessment: Growth active consumers of 255,000 or more in Q1 2027 with marketing at or below 2.4% of sales; supplier income in Growth rising back toward 13% of Growth revenue under the 2027 contracts; general and administrative costs at or below SEK 140m a quarter through mid-2027. What would lower it: marketing back above 2.8%; supplier income flat as a share of sales in Q1 2027; any restriction of supply by a major manufacturer, or a delivery or flavour restriction in a large state.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

C+587th of 24

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: the perimeter is narrow and getting narrower: essentially one product category (oral nicotine; pouches are 72% of volumes), one channel (online), two segments and about eight countries, with the US the only market that really matters for growth. Narrowness makes the business simpler to model and its drivers easier to track, but it concentrates exposure: one regulator (the US), three suppliers and one search platform can each move the whole forecast.

Evidence:

  • Markets: Sweden and Norway (Core, about 68% of Q3 2026 sales), the US, UK, Germany, Switzerland (Growth, about 32%), Saudi Arabia (new); Austria exited; UK and German vape exited.
  • Currency mix: SEK 48%, NOK 22%, USD 20%, GBP 7%, EUR 3% (2025); USD and GBP rising.
  • Predictability effect: high for revenue direction (habitual demand, transparent KPIs such as active consumers, orders, average order value, volume growth, Media and Insights share); low for EPS (discretionary marketing, recurring one-offs, currency noise, binary regulatory and supplier events).
D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

–50

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the expected change is plausible, and on the revenue and gross-profit side it relies only on strengths the business has shown repeatedly. On the cost side it relies on restraint that management has shown when its growth opportunity was constrained (2024) and has not shown when the window was open (2026), and the window is open until at least mid-2027. The thesis therefore needs no new commercial capability; it needs a change of behaviour by the same people, for which the evidence is one admission (the Q3 2026 finding that paid marketing bought customers who would have come anyway), one flat quarter of overhead, and an incentive plan. That is enough to make the 2027 forecast reasonable and not enough to make it reliable. Two things usually counted as weaknesses are stronger than the earlier sets say: the value of the United States customer, and the durability of Nordic supplier income.

What the change is: adjusted EBITDA from SEK 220m (4.6%) in 2026 to SEK 309m (5.6%) and SEK 431m (6.8%); the Growth segment from a loss of about SEK 144m to SEK 105m to SEK 23m.

Strengths demonstrated, and what each carries:

  • Adding gross profit. SEK 150m, 175m and 194m in the last three years; the forecast needs SEK 167m and 179m. Demonstrated in a supply shock (2025) as well as a boom (2026).
  • Adding customers without paying for them. Growth active consumers rose by 80,000 in a year to 227,000 (+55%). About 97% of traffic is unpaid, and management's own test showed the paid part was largely not incremental. The forecast needs about 16,000 net additions a quarter (292,000 by Q3 2027), below the 2026 pace. This is why cutting marketing here is not the experiment it was for the peers in the research report. Chewy, Zalando, Lyko, Boozt and Redcare cut paid marketing and mostly kept the margin gain while growth slowed; 1-800-Flowers lost 36% of demand on the sites it stopped supporting. Those peers spend 6 to 10% of sales on marketing where the files give the figure; Haypp is cutting about one point from about 3%.
  • Supplier income growing faster than sales. About twice the group's growth rate since listing; about 40% in Q3 2026.
  • Building and running the physical and technical platform on time. Four automated warehouses, the systems migration, biometric locker checks within weeks. Texas runs at a third of capacity; fulfilment cost per can fell 6% in Q3 2026. The forecast needs no new capacity.
  • Recovering from shocks. The ZYN pause, the licence dispute, the California exit.

The strength that would have to be acquired, or rather re-acquired:

  • Cost restraint while the opportunity is open. Costs above EBITDA grew by SEK 45m in 2024, when ZYN supply and regulation constrained the United States business and management said it would stay "prudent"; by SEK 148m in 2025 and SEK 211m in 2026, when the constraint lifted. In 2027 the constraint is still lifted: FDA enforcement discretion, 39 newly authorised products since May 2026, ZYN available, the United Kingdom advertising window closing on 1 June 2027 (management: "the window remains open"), a chief executive who wants "as much flexibility as possible to land grab". The forecast needs 2024's behaviour in 2026's conditions. Profit has fallen short of the brokers' expectations in at least four of the last six quarters (16% in Q2 2025, 13% in Q4 2025, 24% in Q1 2026 on Deutsche Bank's account, 12% in Q3 2026), each time on costs management chose.
  • What supports the change: (a) the incrementality finding makes restraint rational rather than forced, because more spending does not buy more growth; (b) general and administrative costs were flat between Q2 and Q3 2026 (about SEK 127m to 133m a quarter), and the 2027 input of SEK 556m already allows 7 to 9% above that run-rate; (c) executives have bought into an incentive plan whose shares convert only above 130% of the issue price (hurdles up to SEK 177.66), and 2028 is the target year for a margin range whose low end (4.0%) is the one target wholly within management's control; (d) from 1 June 2027 the law removes the main paid channels in the United Kingdom.
  • What weakens it: (a) one flat quarter, in which holiday accruals helped; (b) Media and Insights is sold as near-100% gross margin, so the cost of producing it (analysts, studies, the sensory panel launching in 2027) sits in overhead, and an income stream growing by SEK 170m a year is unlikely to need no more people; (c) the two fines argue for more compliance spending in the United States, not less; (d) Saudi Arabia and any further market are new cost before revenue.
  • Size of the dependence: if general and administrative costs grow 15% in 2027 instead of about 9%, EBITDA is about SEK 279m instead of SEK 309m and adjusted EBIT about 16% lower. That is well short of a repeat of 2026.

Two strengths the earlier sets under-rate (my derivations, from orders, average order value and segment revenue in the Q3 2026 report):

  • The Growth customer is already worth about as much as the Core customer. In Q3 2026 revenue per active consumer was SEK 1,713 in Growth and SEK 1,829 in Core. Growth customers order less often (1.8 orders a quarter against 2.3) but spend more per order (SEK 847 against about SEK 697). The first set expected a mature United States margin of 6 to 9% rather than Core's 12% because Americans use 3.6 pouches a day against 10 to 15 in Scandinavia, and the research report calls that gap "the single largest impediment". The gap describes the average American user; it does not describe Haypp's customers, who are the heavy users and pay United States prices. I disagree with the first set on this ground: the evidence does not support a structurally lower margin for reasons of customer value. Growth loses money for a different reason. On my estimate its Q3 2026 gross profit of about SEK 74m bore about SEK 35m of the group's SEK 39m of marketing and about SEK 75m of its SEK 127m of general and administrative costs: 59% of the overhead on 32% of the revenue and a third of the customers. Breakeven is therefore arithmetic. At a contribution of about 12% of revenue after fulfilment and normal marketing, an overhead of about SEK 330m needs revenue of about SEK 2.8bn, which the forecast reaches in 2028 to 2029 (SEK 2.6bn, then 3.3bn), with roughly twice today's customer count. The requirement is more of the same customers on a held cost base: the first half is a demonstrated strength, the second is the open question above.
  • Core's supplier income rests on restrictions, not only on rivalry. Subtracting orders times average order value from segment revenue leaves about SEK 106m in Core and SEK 42m in Growth in Q3 2026, which sums to the SEK 148m of Media and Insights the company reports; the same calculation a year earlier gives about SEK 71m and SEK 33m. If that residual is Media and Insights, 72% of it is earned in Sweden and Norway, where it grew about 49%, and Core's whole EBITDA (SEK 104m) is about equal to it. Norway bans display and, from January 2027, strength and flavour descriptions on cans; Sweden restricts marketing; the United Kingdom bans advertising from June 2027. In those markets Haypp's site is one of the few lawful places a manufacturer can present a product, which is a firmer basis than the discretionary retail-media budgets of the peers in the research report (Criteo's retail-media revenue fell about 20% when its largest client took the work in-house). The other half of the finding cuts the other way: supplier income in Growth grew at half the pace of Growth revenue, so its share of Growth revenue fell from about 13% to about 11%. The likely reason is the calendar: contracts for 2026 were agreed in late 2025, when ZYN had just returned and the United States base was a third smaller. The contracts for 2027, agreed around January 2027, are the first priced on the larger base. If Growth's share only returned to 13%, 2027 EBITDA would be about SEK 45m (15%) higher than forecast. If instead it grows only in line with Growth revenue, the "larger base, more supplier income" loop is not yet working in the United States, and the 30% growth the second set describes would need Nordic supplier income to grow about 28% on Core revenue growing 8%.

Precedents: the nearest is the company's own 2024 (EBITDA doubled on revenue up 16%). Chewy took its EBITDA margin from 3.3% to about 5.6% in two years on overhead absorption and sponsored advertising, close to what is asked of Haypp between 2026 and 2028 (4.6% to 6.8%). The failures in the research had different causes that Haypp does not share (inventory, automation ramps, cyclical demand) or does (a concentrated payer: Criteo; a supplier withdrawing a contract: Scandinavian Tobacco Group's loss of zyn.com, which cut its North American online sales by 10%).

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?–36full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

–36

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: narrow and roughly symmetric for revenue; wide and skewed down for profit; very wide for the share price, where two large opposite forces nearly cancel. In kronor the profit cone is modest, about one point of margin either way. As a share of profit it is about plus or minus 20% on EBITDA and about minus 45% to plus 35% on earnings per share, because the margin is thin. The case is predictable in direction and in the order of events, and not in the level of 2027 earnings. The cone narrows mechanically if the thesis delivers, because the same kronor of noise becomes a smaller share of a larger profit, and widens if it does not.

The cone for 2027 around our forecast (probabilities are my judgements):

  • Revenue SEK 5.52bn; range about SEK 5.2bn to 5.85bn (6% either way). Symmetric over the year. It leans down in the first half: the marketing cut; ZYN's larger can, which I estimate takes SEK 100m to 125m (about 2% of group revenue) off a full year if the can price is unchanged and ZYN is a little over a third of United States sales; and biometric checks at Swedish parcel lockers since October 2026, a new friction in the segment that earns all the profit and one that neither earlier set put a number on. It leans up in the second half: the United Kingdom advertising ban removes rivals' main channel from 1 June 2027; New York's 75% wholesale tax (from 1 September 2026) widens the online price gap; the range keeps widening with FDA authorisations. About half of revenue is in currencies other than the krona and nothing is hedged.
  • Adjusted EBITDA SEK 309m; range about SEK 245m to 370m (4.7% to 6.3% of revenue).
  • Adjusted earnings per share about 4.2. Upper case about 5.6 (18%): Growth revenue up about 42%, marketing below 2%; this is the brokers' median. Base about 4.2 (50%). Lower case about 2.3 (20%): Growth stalls after the cut, management spends again, supplier income slows. Severe event 1.0 to 1.5 (12%): a supplier or a large state restricts supply or delivery. Weighted: about 3.7, which is 11% below the base case, 27% below the brokers' mean (5.09) and 36% below their median (5.80).

Why profit is skewed down when revenue is not:

  1. The record is one-sided. Each of the four comparisons of group profit with consensus, or with the covering broker's estimate, that the files give for the last six quarters is a shortfall, of 12 to 24%.
  2. Management's response is asymmetric. If revenue falls short, costs are cut and profit is partly protected. If revenue runs ahead, the same management treats it as an open window and spends: in 2026 gross profit rose by SEK 194m and costs by SEK 211m. The levers that protect the downside in kronor (marketing, price reinvestment) are the reason revenue upside does not become profit upside. Both tails of profit are cut off, the upper one more.
  3. The tails are one-sided within 18 months. The negative events are sudden (a supplier decision, a state law, a court ruling, a fine). Their positive counterpart, United States online penetration doubling, which Deutsche Bank says would lift 2028 EBITDA by 75 to 150%, works through users' daily consumption rising over years and cannot arrive inside the window.
  4. Below EBITDA the noise has had a sign. Currency revaluation has raised finance costs in each of the last two years; items affecting comparability have risen each year.

Why the cone is narrow in kronor and what follows: the quarterly shortfalls were SEK 4m to 9m of adjusted EBIT (SEK 26.3m against SEK 30m in Q3 2026; SEK 27.2m against about SEK 36m in Q1 2026) on revenue of SEK 1.1bn to 1.2bn, that is 0.3 to 0.8 points of margin. Against a quarterly EBIT of SEK 26m that is 12 to 24%. Against the SEK 76m a quarter forecast for 2028 the same error would be 5 to 12%. Predictability is therefore not fixed: it improves with every point of margin gained and deteriorates with every point lost. That is a reinforcing loop in the share's risk as well as in its earnings.

What is predictable: the order of events. On the base case the year-on-year comparison runs as follows (revenue growth; adjusted EBITDA margin against the same quarter a year earlier):

Report Revenue growth EBITDA margin A year earlier
Q4 2026, 10 February 2027 +18.6% (Q3: +28.2%) 4.6% 5.3%
Q1 2027, early May 2027 +15.8% 4.7% 4.8%
Q2 2027, mid-August 2027 +16.5% 5.3% 4.6%
Q3 2027, early November 2027 +16.3% 6.3% 4.5%
Q4 2027, February 2028 +16.5% 6.0% 4.6%
Q1 2028, May 2028 +15.0% 6.0% 4.7%

The first two reports show slowing growth with a margin below or level with the year before, by construction; the next four show stable growth against easy margin comparisons. If the base case is right, the 18 months divide into six months in which the news looks poor and twelve in which it looks good. I think differently from the first set here: it called the Q4 2026 report the point at which estimate revisions "should either stop or accelerate". That report cannot show a margin gain; it can only fail to disappoint.

The share price. The model's return at the guideline fair multiple is -3.0% at the 18-month column (31 March 2028): -41% from the multiple (24.6 times our two-year-forward earnings today, 14.45 times then) and +65% from earnings. Three points for the Consumer. First, the earnings in that calculation are the two-year-forward figure in March 2028, about 10.4 a share, which is the year to about March 2030 (three-quarters of 2029, one-quarter of 2030). The 18-month return is therefore set by what investors in spring 2028 believe about 2029 and 2030, the part of the forecast in which about 80% of the profit growth arrives and which rests on Growth being in profit. Second, a return that is the small difference between two large numbers is unstable even if each is roughly right: one turn of the multiple is 7% of value (13.3 times, the ten-year median, gives -11%; 16.6 times gives +11%; 11.7 times gives -21%), and 10% less on earnings gives -13%. Third, the plausible range runs from about -45% (the first set's trough of SEK 80 to 95) to about +40% (the two higher broker targets, SEK 210 and 220), around a centre near zero.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

–58

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: helpful, and better than usual for a company of this size, for the purpose of detecting failure; not able to confirm success inside six months. Each quarter the company reports the decisive quantities (EBITDA by segment; active consumers, orders and order value by segment; the Media and Insights share of sales and the split of gross profit between products and Media and Insights; marketing, fulfilment and general and administrative costs as shares of sales) and it publishes the consensus against which it will be judged. Three things limit the value over this particular window: the two reports in it fall in the quarters where the margin comparison cannot show the thesis; three distortions hit the growth indicators at once; and management withholds the plans behind the cost lines.

What arrives by mid-May 2027:

  • Company: the Q4 2026 report (10 February 2027), each preceded by the consensus the company compiles through Modular Finance; the annual report (the last one came on 28 April), which carries the tax note with unrecognised losses, the schedule of acquired intangibles and the loans within the group; the Q1 2027 report (early May).
  • Outside the company: Philip Morris and Altria report before Haypp does, with the first evidence on ZYN's can change and on promotions; four-weekly United States scanner data on pouch volume, price and brand share; the Swedish Retail Federation's e-commerce indicator, which reports the online nicotine category; search interest for Nicokick and Northerner, which Deutsche Bank uses as a lead indicator of Growth revenue; the United States state sessions (January to March 2027); the European Commission's tobacco products proposal (expected early 2027). The United Kingdom advertising ban (1 June 2027) falls just outside.

What to read, in my order of reliability for this window, with the level that would be consistent with the base case:

  1. Growth active consumers (227,000 in Q3 2026). Unaffected by can size, price or currency. Base case: about 16,000 more a quarter, so about 240,000 or more in Q4 2026 and 255,000 or more in Q1 2027. Under 235,000 in Q4 would be a stall after the marketing cut.
  2. Growth orders per active consumer (1.8 a quarter). If the larger ZYN can lengthens the interval between orders, this falls first, before revenue shows it.
  3. Marketing as a share of sales (3.2% in Q3 2026; base case 2.4% in Q4 and 2.3% in Q1), with general and administrative costs no higher than about SEK 135m in Q4 2026 and SEK 140m in Q1 2027. Marketing above 2.8% means the cut did not happen.
  4. The Media and Insights share of revenue against a year earlier, and, from the derivation in D-02, the part earned in Growth (segment revenue less orders times order value: about SEK 42m in Q3 2026). The Q1 2027 report is the first under the 2027 contracts and the most informative single number in the window.
  5. Segment EBITDA. Growth's loss narrower than Q3's SEK 48m (base case about SEK 38m in Q4); Core at about SEK 95m or more.
  6. Group revenue growth (base case +18.6%, then +15.8%). The least informative, because of the base effect; see I-02 for what the brokers expect.

What not to use: pouch volume growth counted in cans (the first set's threshold of 50% for United States and United Kingdom volumes), which ZYN's change from 15 to 20 pouches distorts from Q4 2026 to Q3 2027, as the company itself has said; quarterly cash flow and net debt, which swing by SEK 90m to 180m a quarter on timing; reported earnings per share and the reported tax rate (102% in Q2 2026, -11% for nine months).

What is missing, and what it costs: no annual guidance; no marketing budget (management declined to quantify the Q4 cut); no split of the United States from the United Kingdom beyond volume growth; nothing on Media and Insights by segment, by customer or by contract; no customer acquisition cost; the city and the alleged breach behind the USD 2.5m provision withheld. An investor can therefore tell within a quarter whether Growth customers are still arriving and whether costs are held, but not why if they are not, and cannot judge the next quarter's spending in advance.

The asymmetry that matters: over the next six months the disclosure can show the thesis failing (a stall in Growth customers, marketing back above 2.8%, supplier income flat as a share of sales). It cannot show it succeeding, because even a perfect Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 leave the margin below or level with a year earlier. For six months, monitoring is a watch for negative evidence.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

–50

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the trace is clear and unusually short from operational drivers to adjusted EBITDA; loose from the company's stated targets to those drivers; and broken between adjusted EBIT and earnings per share. The external transmission is clear but runs through a channel the earlier sets did not isolate: value-chain events reach earnings mainly through what manufacturers spend on Haypp, and hardly at all through product revenue, because a krona of product revenue leaves about 6 to 7 ore of contribution and a krona of supplier income leaves nearly all of it.

From targets to drivers (loose): the company states where revenue growth should come from (the United States about 65% of the increase to 2028; its share of the total United States pouch market from 2 to 3% to 4 to 5%). It states no path for marketing, overhead, Media and Insights or segment margins. The margin target is three points wide; on SEK 6.3bn of revenue that is SEK 253m to SEK 443m of EBIT, a range that says almost nothing about earnings per share.

From drivers to adjusted EBITDA (clear): five reported quantities rebuild the income statement. For Q3 2026: 1.452m orders at SEK 739 give SEK 1,073m of product revenue; adding SEK 148m of Media and Insights gives the SEK 1,221m reported; gross profit is SEK 93m on products plus SEK 148m; less marketing (SEK 39m), fulfilment (about SEK 21m) and general and administrative costs (about SEK 127m) leaves about SEK 55m, the reported adjusted EBITDA, and the same can be done by segment. Few companies allow a forecast to be re-based line by line each quarter from published figures. This is the main support for predictability above EBITDA.

From adjusted EBIT to earnings per share (broken): four items stand between them and none is forecastable quarter to quarter. Items affecting comparability (SEK 30m in 2024, 53m in 2025, about 65m in 2026); acquisition amortisation (about SEK 36m to 40m a year); currency revaluation inside finance costs (net finance cost SEK 5m, 12.5m, about 26m); and tax (36%, 24%, 7.5%, then -11% for nine months of 2026). Reported earnings per share were under 42% of the brokers' adjusted figure in each of 2023 to 2025 and are negative in 2026. Adjusted earnings per share itself depends on conventions that differ between us, the company and the brokers: the share count (about 31.1m basic on the company's figures, 31.8m at FactSet) and the tax rate (Deutsche Bank uses a normalised 20.6%). For 2027 one year has three earnings figures: about 4.2 adjusted; about 3.5 if the recurring items affecting comparability are treated as the operating costs they have been for three years (SEK 30m, about 0.7 a share after tax); and about 2.2 to 2.5 reported. Valuation, ours and the brokers', uses the highest.

The external transmission:

  • The two rates. Product revenue carries a gross margin of about 8.7% and fulfilment of about 2%: SEK 100m more or less of it moves EBITDA by SEK 6m to 7m. Supplier income carries almost no direct cost: SEK 100m of it moves EBITDA by about SEK 100m.
  • So the events that move revenue most move profit least. ZYN's larger can takes perhaps SEK 100m to 125m off revenue and about SEK 7m to 8m off EBITDA. Manufacturer-funded promotions, excise pass-through and currency on product sales work the same way: large on the growth rate, small on profit.
  • And the events that move profit most are upstream. Manufacturers' rivalry, launches and restrictions on other advertising set the Media and Insights contracts, which are agreed once a year for the calendar year, after the customer base they are priced on. The lead indicators for Haypp's earnings are therefore the number of manufacturers fighting for share (Velo from 16% to about 30% of United States volume), the number of new products authorised (39 since May 2026, plus 24 for Velo), and advertising restrictions that leave Haypp's site as one of the few lawful channels. The opposite signals are consolidation among brand owners or one brand becoming dominant.
  • Taxes and prices in shops reach Haypp with a lag: a state tax raises the shelf price, heavy users move online, the customer base grows, and supplier income follows up to a year later.
  • Supply permission is the one direct, immediate link: it removes product revenue, customers and supplier income together.

What this means for reading the next four quarters: investors treat revenue as Haypp's reliable line, yet it is the line most distorted by things that hardly touch profit (the base effect, the can, promotions, currency), while the line that drives earnings is fixed early in the year by contract. Weak reported revenue with supplier income and costs on track would be a false alarm; revenue on track with supplier income flat as a share of sales would be a false comfort.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

D3317th of 24

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: sentiment risk is high: the shares sit near the top of their own valuation range, are illiquid, are covered by only three brokers, are excluded by many Nordic funds, and react sharply to regulatory headlines even when the direct impact is small (as in late 2024 and late 2025). The current market regime (risk-off beneath an index high; small-cap fragility at Double Underweight) adds a general de-rating risk unrelated to Haypp's results.

Main channels:

  • Regulatory headlines that are scary but slow: the eight-state EU letter, a TPD3 draft (early 2027), UK plain-packaging consultations, US FDA statements, youth-use reports.
  • Peer and supplier events: PMI or BAT commentary on pouch pricing, promotions or online channels; Velo or ZYN price-war headlines; a manufacturer announcing a direct-to-consumer platform.
  • Earnings-quality shocks: another quarter of adjusted-profit miss or a new legal provision with reported losses, at an 83rd to 87th percentile multiple.
  • Market structure: about USD 0.4 to 0.5m of shares traded a day; no options market; a few large holders; Pareto's Hold (SEK 160) and dashboard sell-side momentum at D; small-cap growth stocks "crack before large caps" in risk-off regimes (Wisdom Library).
  • Theme rotation: the Sector Digest notes Haypp as the only premium multiple in a sector otherwise priced at 6 to 10 times earnings; a rotation into cheap, buyback-heavy manufacturers would leave Haypp behind.

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?F4822nd of 24full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

F3819th of 24

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: there is no record of upgrading guidance. The pattern is the opposite: ambitious long-dated targets are set at capital markets days, reiterated each quarter, and, when missed, replaced by new targets with a later date (the SEK 5bn 2025 target set before and reiterated at the November 2023 CMD, replaced by the 2028 targets at the April 2025 CMD). The CEO's behaviour is optimistic on timing and candid about past mistakes in unscripted settings.

Evidence:

  • 2023 CMD: "2025 financial targets confirmed" with extra investment in adjacencies; 2025 actual sales SEK 3.85bn against SEK 5bn.
  • April 2025: new targets that DNB Carnegie called "much more bullish than anticipated"; reiterated in every quarter since, including Q3 2026 ("on track").
  • No interim upgrades or downgrades; profit expectations have been reset by the brokers, not by the company.
  • In unscripted Q&A, the CEO refused to promise ZYN restock dates, admitted a "graveyard full" of capital-allocation mistakes and conceded the 2025 target was unachievable; this candour is positive, but it has not translated into guidance discipline.
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

F3822nd of 24

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: over one year, revenue has met or slightly beaten estimates while profit has repeatedly missed; over three years, the company met the market's expectations in 2024 (a harvest year) and missed badly in 2025 (supply shock) and 2026 (investment year); over five years since its 2021 listing, it missed its only dated sales target (SEK 5bn by 2025) by 23%. In comparable conditions to the next 18 months (heavy investment in Growth), the record is of profit misses.

Evidence:

  • Last year (quarter by quarter, where the files give the comparison): Q4 2025 earnings 13% below consensus (DNB Carnegie); Q1 2026 adjusted EBIT margin down 270 basis points, followed by 12% cuts to FY2026 EPS; Q3 2026 revenue SEK 1,220.9m against SEK 1,212m consensus (beat by about 1%), gross margin 19.7% against 19.3% (beat), adjusted EBIT SEK 26.3m against SEK 30m (12.3% miss; 2% above Deutsche Bank's SEK 25.7m), EPS -1.39 against +0.40 (large miss, provisions), Core EBITDA about 15% above Deutsche Bank. FY2026 consensus EPS fell 73% over twelve months.
  • Three years: 2024: brokers' EPS +46% with "proven strong sales and earnings momentum" (DNB Carnegie, August 2024); 2025: Q2 organic growth of 1% against a 4% estimate, adjusted EBITA 16% below, full-year sales far below target; 2026: growth beats, profit misses.
  • Five years: listed 2021; negative free cash flow in 2022 and 2023 during integration; the 2025 target missed; the 2028 targets now in place.
  • Pattern: revenue is forecastable and usually met; profit misses cluster around expansion spending and one-off items, i.e., around management's discretionary choices.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

F4015th of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: weight the revenue forecasts heavily, the EBITDA forecasts moderately and the EPS forecasts lightly. Lean revenue down against the company's 2028 target, but not against the current run-rate. Lean profit later rather than lower over four years. Lean reported earnings and cash firmly down against adjusted earnings. The record is consistent: revenue has been forecastable and usually met, except when a supplier removed a brand. Profit has missed in every investment phase, and the misses cluster around the items management controls: marketing, the Growth cost base and "one-off" charges. My base case already embeds these leans, which is why it sits below the median broker on every line from 2027. The one place where the record argues for leaning up is Core and gross margin, which have beaten. I do not lean up there, because part of 2026's beat came from non-recurring pass-through and stock gains.

Reasoning:

  • Revenue record. Q3 2026 revenue SEK 1,220.9m against the company-compiled consensus of SEK 1,212m (+0.7%); 2026 constant-currency growth of 24%, 27% and 26% by quarter, in line with or above brokers. FY2027 consensus sales were essentially unchanged over twelve months (-1%), while EPS was cut 47 to 53%, which shows the market already treats revenue as the reliable line. The exception is supply: Q2 2025 organic growth was 1% against a 4% estimate, and the SEK 5bn 2025 target was missed by 23% (actual SEK 3.85bn). Weight: high for 2026 Q4 and 2027 (my 2027 revenue cone is ±6%); medium for 2028 (±10%); directional for 2029 to 2030.
  • Profit record. Q2 2025 adjusted EBITA 16% below DNB Carnegie; Q4 2025 earnings 13% below consensus; Q1 2026 adjusted EBIT margin down 270 basis points, followed by 12% EPS cuts; Q3 2026 adjusted EBIT SEK 26.3m against the company-compiled SEK 30m (-12.3%; 2% above Deutsche Bank's SEK 25.7m). That is at least four profit misses in the last six reported quarters. FY2026 consensus EPS has fallen 73% in twelve months and FY2027 by 53%. Weight: medium for 2027 EBITDA (cone ±20%), low for 2028 (±30%).
  • EPS and reported earnings. Reported EPS has never exceeded about 42% of brokers' adjusted EPS in 2023 to 2025 (0.17 against 2.48; 1.51 against 3.62; 1.39 against 4.00). Items affecting comparability recur every year (legal, restructuring, incentive-related bonuses), and the tax rate has been erratic (35.9%, 23.7%, 7.5%, -11%). Weight on EPS: low (2027 cone about ±35%, 2028 about ±45%).
  • Direction of the lean and its size. Against the company's targets: lean down on revenue (my 2028 is 11% below the floor) and on absolute EBIT (my 2.25 times 2024 against three to four times). Against the margin target: none needed (4.8% is inside the range). Against consensus: my 2027 revenue is 4.8% below, EBITDA 7% below and EPS 29% below. Against the first set: my 2027 EPS of 4.13 is below its central 4.5 to 5.0 because of the tax rate (25% rather than Deutsche Bank's normalised 20.6%) and a higher finance cost, and it is close to the Hold broker's path the first set called "the more realistic shape". My 2028 EPS of 6.87 is below all three brokers (Pareto 8.02). That gap comes mainly from revenue, and I would close it if Growth revenue holds sequential growth above about 9% a quarter after the marketing cut.
  • Skew after the lean. Even after leaning, the 2027 EPS distribution is skewed down: bear 2.3 (15%), base 4.1 (55%), bull 5.6 (20%), and a supplier or regulatory tail of 1.0 to 1.5 (10%). The probability-weighted value is about 3.9, below my base. The bull case equals the consensus median: consensus is priced for my bull case in 2027.
  • Where the record does not apply. Operational delivery (ERP and storefront migration, four automated warehouses, the ZYN restart, biometric lockers within weeks) has been strong and on time. The Consumer can trust the platform to handle volume; the forecast risk is commercial and financial, not operational.

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Haypp's next four years are likely to deliver strong, durable revenue growth and a large, back-end-loaded margin recovery. Adjusted EPS more than triples from 2026 to 2028 and adjusted EBIT more than quadruples by 2030 on my base case. But that base case sits clearly below consensus: about 5% on 2027 revenue, 7% on EBITDA and 29% on EPS, widening to about 8%, 14% and 27% in 2028. It also sits below the company's own 2028 revenue and absolute-EBIT ambitions. The engine is sound and mostly mechanical. A fixed G&A and automation base built in 2025 to 2026 is filled by habitual, migrating demand, and supplier-paid Media and Insights income funds both the price advantage and the profit. The forecast's weaknesses are concentrated rather than diffuse. (1) The Growth segment must keep growing about 30 to 35% a year while paid marketing falls; that is the single decisive driver. (2) Media and Insights must keep growing about twice the group rate while manufacturers talk of cutting trade budgets; that is the largest and least-discussed sensitivity. (3) Below EBIT, a tax rate above statutory, recurring "one-off" costs, acquisition amortisation and capitalised software keep reported EPS at about 60% of adjusted and FCF at about 40% of adjusted net income in 2027, before scale closes the gap from 2028. The marketing cut announced for Q4 2026 matters more as an exit rate and a downside cushion than as a source of 2027 margin gain. The 2027 recovery comes mainly from G&A absorption. Track record argues for heavy weight on revenue and light weight on EPS, and my base already embeds the lean. Even so, the 2027 EPS distribution is skewed down, and the consensus median equals my bull case. For the third and fourth sets: the case is predictable in direction and timing of revenue, much less in the level and timing of profit. The Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 reports will mainly test revenue resilience after the marketing cut; the Q2 and Q3 2027 reports test the margin step-up.

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

F4412th of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: below-operating-profit items will keep reported EPS and free cash flow well below adjusted earnings in 2026 and 2027, then the gap will narrow mechanically from 2028, because the leakage items are roughly fixed in krona while operating profit compounds. Reported EPS goes from about 59% of my adjusted EPS (2027) to about 77% (2028) and about 92% (2030), against under 42% in every year from 2023 to 2025. Full FCF goes from about 37% of adjusted net income (2027) to about 73% (2028) and 86% (2030). Four items do the damage: items affecting comparability (SEK 20 to 30m a year, recurring and cash), acquisition amortisation (about SEK 38m a year, non-cash, outside adjusted earnings), a tax rate above the Swedish statutory 20.6% while the US and UK lose money (my 28%, 25%, 24%, 23%, 22%), and capitalised software of about SEK 90m a year that never passes through EBITDA. There are no minorities, associates, dividends or buybacks; dilution is about 0.9% a year. Against Deutsche Bank, tax alone is worth about SEK 0.25 to 0.35 of EPS a year in 2027 and 2028.

Reasoning:

  • Items affecting comparability (history: SEK 30.4m in 2024, 52.6m in 2025, about 65m in 2026). The 2026 figure is mostly Q3's SEK 52.7m: SEK 23.0m of restructuring and incentive-related bonuses, SEK 6.3m of consulting, and the USD 2.5m (about SEK 23.5m) US provision. US legal costs have now recurred for three years (San Francisco SEK 18.1m in 2025; the unnamed city in 2026). Cash bonuses that fund executives' co-investment have recurred twice (SEK 14.8m in 2024; 2026). I carry SEK 30m (2027, plus payment of the 2026 provision), SEK 25m (2028) and SEK 20m a year after that. Treat them as operating costs that management has labelled one-off.
  • Acquisition amortisation. About SEK 35 to 42m a year (Northerner and smaller deals). Adjusted EPS (mine and the brokers') excludes it; reported EPS does not. I assume about SEK 38m a year in 2027 and 2028, falling to about SEK 30m (2029) and SEK 20m (2030) as the 2019 Northerner intangibles approach full amortisation. That timing is my assumption; the files give no schedule.
  • Net financial items (P-02): SEK -26m (2026) falling to SEK -3m (2030); currency revaluation ±SEK 15m a year is the noise band.
  • Tax: the least appreciated EPS input. The Swedish and Norwegian entities that earn the profit pay 20.6% and 22%. Local US and UK losses probably create no recognised deferred tax asset: the company recognises one only when future surpluses are "probable", and the 9M 2026 reported rate was -11% because currency effects sit in other comprehensive income. Evidence for a rate above statutory: 23.7% in 2024, when Growth was near breakeven; the consensus pre-tax to net-income lines imply about 28% (2027) and 25% (2028). Deutsche Bank normalises at 20.6%. My path: 28% (2026), 25% (2027), 24% (2028), 23% (2029), 22% (2030), falling as Growth turns profitable and the US and UK statutory rates (about 25%) blend in. Upside: recognising US and UK loss carry-forwards once Growth is sustainably profitable (2029 on my model) would give a one-off boost to reported profit and a lower ongoing rate. DNB Carnegie's November 2024 precedent: a Swedish deferred-tax release lifted its 2024 EPS estimate by 12%.
  • Share count: +0.5m (2026, warrant tender), +0.15m, +0.15m, +0.4m (LTIP conversion), +0.2m. No buybacks, no cash issuance. Net buybacks of zero against peers in the sector buying back 8 to 13% of market value a year: Haypp's EPS growth must come entirely from operations.
  • Resulting EPS and cash (mine). Adjusted EPS 1.88, 4.13, 6.87, 9.54, 12.62. Reported EPS about -1.0, 2.5, 5.3, 8.3, 11.6. Last six years of reported EPS: -1.19 (2021), 0.69, 0.17, 1.51, 1.39 (2025). Full FCF about SEK 43m, 49m, 162m, 255m, 361m, against negative in 2022 and 2023, about SEK 63m (2024) and SEK 22m (2025) on Deutsche Bank's full definition. FactSet's own FCF definition (operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex only: SEK 160m in 2024, 130m in 2025) flatters by excluding about SEK 90m of capitalised software and SEK 30 to 40m of lease payments. Do not use it.
  • 2026 cash-flow caution. Nine-month 2026 operating cash flow of SEK 212.8m included a SEK 112.8m working-capital inflow: lower inventory after the late-2025 stock build, plus higher deferred revenue, mainly manufacturers' Media and Insights prepayments under annual contracts. Accrued but unpaid items (the US provision, bonus accruals) also helped. Part of this reverses in Q4 and in 2027. Treat 2026 cash flow as flattered, and do not extrapolate it.

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

A701st of 24

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: the scope is large and concentrated in one big area: moving the Growth segment from about -12% EBITDA margin toward the Core segment's 10 to 12.5%. That single change, on Growth revenue of SEK 3 to 4bn by 2029 to 2030, is worth roughly SEK 500 to 600m of EBITDA swing. The precedent is Haypp's own: Core moved from about 8.6% to 12.5% EBITDA as it matured. Smaller contributors are Media and Insights mix, warehouse utilisation and G&A leverage. The improvement is the strategy itself, so it is fully congruent.

Reasoning:

  • The big area (Growth margin). Every 1 percentage point of Growth EBITDA margin on 2027 consensus Growth revenue (about SEK 2.17bn) is about SEK 22m of EBITDA (about 7% of group EBITDA); at 2028 Growth revenue (about SEK 2.98bn) about SEK 30m. Consensus already assumes Growth reaches about breakeven in 2028; Deutsche Bank models Growth at -4.5% in 2027 and +0.3% in 2028, and the 2028 group target (5.5% EBIT) requires little more. Upside is the path beyond 2028.
  • Precedent quality. The Nordic market reached maturity with 36% online penetration and 80 to 90% share; the US and UK are at about 3 and 7% online penetration. The structural difference is lower daily consumption (US 3.6 to 4 pouches against 10 to 12), which lowers order frequency per customer and so raises fulfilment and acquisition cost per krona of revenue. I would expect the mature US margin to settle below Core's, perhaps 6 to 9% EBITDA, not 12%.
  • Smaller areas: Media and Insights growing faster than product (about 40% against 25%) adds roughly 0.3 to 0.5 percentage points of gross margin a year if sustained; Texas and Milton Keynes utilisation; G&A as a share of sales falling from about 10.4% toward 8 to 9%.
  • Peers: Deutsche Bank models adjusted EBIT growing at about 36% a year from 2025 to 2030 (roughly SEK 700m by 2030 from SEK 150.7m), a 7% margin on about SEK 10bn of revenue.
C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

C668th of 24

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: no; over the last twelve months the growth engine has accelerated sharply (from 7% organic in 2025 to 24 to 27% at constant currency in each quarter of 2026), well above the company's normal rate of about 15 to 20%, and the input force (US and UK online migration) is not slowing. But the acceleration is partly a base effect, and the leading indicators have begun to decelerate sequentially, so a visible slowdown in reported growth from Q4 2026 is likely and should not be misread as the engine failing.

Evidence:

  • Accelerating: group constant-currency growth Q1 2026 about 24%, Q2 about 27% organic, Q3 25.7% (despite about 2 points of drag from the UK vape exit); Growth segment +53% organic in Q3; US and UK pouch volumes +91% and +93%.
  • Normal rate: 2021 to 2025 revenue CAGR about 14%; 2019 to 2025 about 30% (early-stage); 2022 to 2024 annual growth 15 to 22%.
  • Input force: US online penetration about 3%, UK about 7%; UK volume growth more than twice the market; FDA approvals and state taxes supportive.
  • Sequential slowing signals: Growth pouch volume growth 83% (Q1 2026) to 63% (Q3); Core pouch volumes +20% (Q2) to +14% (Q3); Growth active consumers +54.7% with sequential deceleration noted by Deutsche Bank; Swedish online basket values down.
  • Base effect: ZYN returned on 11 September 2025, so Q4 2026 is the first quarter compared against a full ZYN quarter. Core growth also benefits until December 2026 from the January 2026 Swedish excise change and manufacturer price rises.
  • What would mean real deceleration: US or UK pouch volume growth below 50% in 2027 (a threshold named in the latest Earnings Review), Growth active consumer growth below 30%, or Core organic growth below 5% for two quarters.

US online penetration rising from about 3%, UK consolidation after the June 2027 advertising ban, and Media and Insights growth sustain it; ZYN's 20-pouch can (through Q3 2027), promotions and lower paid acquisition hold it back. Group: +23.4%, +16.3%, +14.7%, +13.7%, +12.5%.

The first set's estimate of a mature US margin of 6 to 9% is a post-2030 figure on my path.

  • Cash. Capex SEK 108 to 122m a year (1.5 to 2.6% of sales), lease principal SEK 33 to 45m, working capital about 4.5% to 3.6% of revenue on FactSet's definition (a small outflow each year). Full FCF moves from about SEK 43m (2026) to 361m (2030), and the company becomes net cash, excluding leases, of about SEK 0.9bn by 2030.

Within Growth, the swing depends on fixed-cost absorption of a G&A base of roughly SEK 330 to 350m. The Consumer should treat the Growth margin path as the one driver that decides the forecast.
  • Versus the last four to six years, in one line. Before 2026 Haypp grew fast and earned little. 2026 is its investment trough. From 2027 the model says it keeps growing fast and starts to earn, which the company has never shown at group level for more than two years in a row.

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Judgement: little of what is favourable in our assessment is missing from expectations, and most of what is cautious in it is. Guidance is very unlikely to be raised and will probably be re-dated. The median broker's forecast sits at management's floor on revenue, and ours is below it by 6% on 2027 revenue, 13% on EBITDA and 28% on earnings per share. The one detailed narrative in print is a bull case that counts our main risks as strengths. The gap between our assessment and what is reflected is unfavourable for the next six to twelve months and roughly neutral beyond that, when a lower consensus and easy margin comparisons could allow the first upgrades.

How the three answers fit: the company guides by ambition and re-dates (I-01); the broker that sets the median has adopted the ambition's floor and moved the recovery into 2027 instead of lowering it (I-02); and the published narrative explains why that is reasonable while leaving out the quantities that will decide whether it happens (I-03). Expectations are thus mutually supporting and rest on few independent views: one management, one broker. Our forecast differs in size and timing, not in direction. That limits the damage of being wrong, and it also means our case holds no favourable surprise for the next two reports: the items we see and the market does not are mostly reasons for the numbers to come down first.

The qualification the Consumer should carry: the operating disagreement is smaller than the headline. For 2027 our EBITDA is level with Pareto's and 13% below Deutsche Bank's; three-fifths of the earnings gap is operations and two-fifths is convention. If Q4 2026 revenue comes in near the brokers' SEK 1,295m and supplier income in Growth steps up under the 2027 contracts, it is our forecast that moves up, not theirs that comes down. I give that upper case about 18%.

For the memo's topics:

  • Guidance and the record of raising it (topic 4). Absent. The targets are not conservative, have never been raised, and group profit has fallen short of expectations in at least four of the last six quarters.
  • Scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months (topic 7). Low. Downgrades at the median are more likely first; upgrades need the upper case.
  • Present momentum (topic 8). The dashboard rates sell-side earnings momentum D, technical momentum C (price 13% above the 200-day average; 12-month return +9.1%; relative strength at the 60th percentile), valuation F and the tobacco theme A. Target prices are rising while earnings estimates fall. The peers offer no lead: British American Tobacco D, C and F on the same three ratings; Imperial D, F and B; Scandinavian Tobacco C, C and A; and Haypp's price does not move with theirs (D-07).
  • Place in the cycle (topic 10). The business is turning up, unconfirmed until mid-August 2027. Expectations are set as if the thesis were already broadening. That mismatch is the main risk to the shares over the next six months.
  • The fair multiple (topic 11). The guideline's 14.45 times lies between the ten-year median (13.3 times) and the 75th percentile (16.6 times). I think that is right for a turning-up phase with an earnings cone skewed down. Arguments for more: a growth runway of three to five years, a mature segment that funds it, no debt. Arguments for less: adjusted earnings exclude costs that have recurred for three years (including them lowers 2027 earnings by about 17% and 2030 by about 4%); profit answers to three suppliers; the shares are illiquid; and past troughs were 8 to 10 times. I would not argue for more than the guideline before the August 2027 evidence.
  • The return (topic 12). At the guideline multiple the model gives -3.0% to the 18-month column and +30.3% (9.6% a year) to the three-year column; after my input changes about zero and about +35%. There are no dividends. The whole return is earnings growth set against a fall in the multiple from 24.6 times. The 18-month figure rests on earnings for the year to about March 2030. The three-year figure rests on earnings of about 14 a share for the year to about September 2031, beyond the last year of the inputs (12.80 in 2030), so it contains the model's own extrapolation and should be quoted with that caution.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

–36

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: there is no annual guidance to raise or cut, so the guidance events of the next 18 months are six occasions on which the 2028 targets are repeated or not, and the informal commentary around them. At each of the four reports of 2027 the most likely outcome by far is repetition ("on track"): about 97% in February, falling to about 92% in November. A raise is very unlikely at any point (about 4% in total); the company has never raised a target. I put at about 40% the chance that the revenue target is re-dated, replaced or qualified by May 2028, concentrated at the Q4 2027 report (February 2028) or a capital markets day, because by then the arithmetic is public: on our forecast 2028 would need about 26% growth at constant currency to reach the floor. The margin target is likely to be met at its low end and the EBIT ambition missed. The guidance that can move estimates within six months is informal: what management says on 10 February 2027 about spending in the first half.

The arithmetic of the three targets (set April 2025, repeated at Q3 2026):

  • Revenue: 18 to 25% a year at constant currency from SEK 3,680m in 2024, so SEK 7,134m or more in 2028. Constant-currency growth has been about 7% (2025) and about 24% (2026), which leaves about 21% a year needed in 2027 and 2028. Our forecast (16.3%, then 14.7%) ends about 9% short of the floor. The two brokers together (21%, then 18.6%) end about 2% short. Deutsche Bank alone (about 22%, then 20%) ends exactly on the floor: the broker that sets the median has taken management's minimum as its forecast.
  • Adjusted EBIT margin of 5.5% plus or minus 1.5 points. Ours 4.8%; the two brokers about 5.3%; Deutsche Bank 5.6%. Inside the range on every view. The low end (4.0%) is the one target wholly in management's hands, through marketing, and 2028 is also the year the incentive plan looks to.
  • Three to four times 2024's adjusted EBIT (SEK 404m to 538m). Ours SEK 303m (2.25 times); the two brokers SEK 363m (2.7 times); Deutsche Bank about SEK 394m (2.9 times). No published forecast reaches it. One of the company's three stated aims is already disbelieved by everyone, without comment.

The record, which is the base rate: the previous target (SEK 5bn of sales in 2025, set at listing in 2021 when sales were SEK 1.7bn) was confirmed at the November 2023 capital markets day, missed by 23%, and replaced in April 2025, inside its own target year, by larger and later targets. Targets here are never raised and never cut in advance; they are re-dated late. That pattern points to 2028 itself, at the edge of the 18-month window. A re-dating need not be read badly: Deutsche Bank already models about SEK 10bn of revenue and about SEK 700m of EBIT for 2030, so 2030 targets on that scale would look like progress.

By event (my probabilities):

Event Repeated unchanged Re-dated, replaced or qualified Raised
Q4 2026, 10 February 2027 97% 2% 1%
Q1 2027, early May 2027 96% 3% 1%
Q2 2027, mid-August 2027 95% 4% 1%
Q3 2027, early November 2027 92% 7% 1%
Q4 2027, February 2028 78% 21% 1%
Q1 2028, May 2028 90% 9% 1%

Each row is the chance at that event if the targets still stand when it arrives. Cumulatively, by May 2028: still standing unchanged about 57%; re-dated or replaced about 29%; explicitly lowered about 10%; raised about 4%. The chance of a capital markets day inside the window is about even (the last two were November 2023 and April 2025; none is announced).

What would drive each outcome:

  • Raised: Growth revenue up about 45% through 2027 with the Growth loss inside 3% of its revenue. The company has not raised a target in five years as a listed company, prefers wide ranges and gains nothing by narrowing them.
  • Repeated: the default. The ranges are wide, the basis is constant currency, the margin floor is controllable, and repeating costs nothing until the target year.
  • Re-dated or lowered: the arithmetic once 2027 revenue is reported; a supplier or regulatory shock (the 2025 target was abandoned after the ZYN shortage); or a capital markets day that moves attention to 2030.

The informal guidance on 10 February 2027, in order of weight for estimates: (1) spending in the first half of 2027. There is a reason to spend: the United Kingdom advertising window closes on 1 June 2027, and the second set already allows marketing of 2.4% of sales in Q2 2027. If "elevated through mid-2027" becomes "through 2027", brokers will lower the margin. (2) Whether the lower-funnel cut happened and what it did to customer additions. (3) Anything on the 2027 supplier contracts. (4) The settlement of the United States provision. I put the chance that the commentary leads brokers to lower 2027 margin assumptions at about 35%, to raise them at about 15%, and to leave them at about 50%.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years: the 2028 targets fall due inside this window (the year is reported in February 2029). Revenue floor missed: about 70%. Margin range met: about 70%, at its low end. EBIT ambition missed: about 80%. New targets for 2030 in place by early 2029: about 80%.

What this means for the gap: guidance here is not conservative and has no record of being raised, so there is no mechanism by which management leads expectations up. But with no annual guidance there is also little guidance to cut before 2028, and because even the bullish broker sits only at the revenue floor and none believes the EBIT ambition, a miss of the targets is partly expected. The gap is unfavourable, mildly.

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: more likely cut than raised, at the median and on revenue, over the next two or three reports; then, if the base case holds, stable from a lower level from late 2027, with upgrades possible only in the upper case. Our forecast is below the two brokers on every line and about 27% below the median earnings per share in both 2027 and 2028. About three-fifths of that gap is a difference of view on operations; the rest is convention (tax rate, depreciation, share count) and will never appear as a miss. I put at about 55% the chance that the 2027 median (5.80) is lower after the February report (held 30%, raised 15%), and at about 70% that it is lower by the August report, by 10 to 20%. The mean will fall less, because one broker is already below us. The first test falls on revenue, the line where consensus has not moved in a year. And the estimates have been reset less than the revision statistics suggest.

Where the brokers are, and where we are against each (2027; 2028):

  • Deutsche Bank (Buy, target SEK 220): revenue SEK 5,863m; 7,030m. EBITDA SEK 354m; 507m. Earnings per share 5.80; 9.74. It sets the median for 2026 and 2027.
  • Pareto Securities (Hold, SEK 160): revenue SEK 5,742m; 6,732m. EBITDA SEK 311m; 491m. Earnings per share 3.58; 8.02.
  • Barclays (Overweight, SEK 210): earnings per share only, 5.89; 9.42.
  • Our forecast: revenue SEK 5,524m; 6,336m (6% and 10% below Deutsche Bank, 4% and 6% below Pareto). EBITDA SEK 309m; 431m (13% and 15% below Deutsche Bank; level with Pareto in 2027 and 12% below in 2028). Earnings per share about 4.2; about 7.0 (28% below Deutsche Bank in both years; 17% above Pareto in 2027 and 13% below in 2028).
  • So for 2027 our operating forecast is Pareto's. The consensus median is one broker above both. For 2028 we are below all three, on revenue.

What the 2027 gap to the median is made of (5.80 against about 4.2, a gap of about 1.6): EBITDA SEK 45m lower, about 1.05; depreciation about SEK 8m to 10m higher than Deutsche Bank's, about 0.2; tax at 25% against its normalised 20.6%, about 0.3; a higher share count, about 0.1. Only the first is a view that results can settle. This matters for how the Consumer words the gap: "27% below consensus earnings" overstates the disagreement; "13% below the median broker's EBITDA and level with the other's" states it.

The sequence I expect:

  1. 10 February 2027 (Q4 2026). The two brokers' full-year revenue (SEK 4,797m) less the nine months reported (SEK 3,502m) implies Q4 revenue of about SEK 1,295m, up 23% (Deutsche Bank about SEK 1,306m, Pareto about SEK 1,283m). Ours is SEK 1,248m, up 18.6%: about 4% lower. Their implied Q4 EBITDA (about SEK 57m) equals ours. Most of the revenue difference is in Core: the brokers' full-year Core revenue (SEK 3,300m to 3,321m) is SEK 50m to 70m above the second set's (SEK 3,252m), which assumes the year-earlier quarter was swollen by stocking ahead of the January 2026 price and excise changes. If we are right, the report shows revenue about 4% light with profit in line: the reverse of the pattern of the past year, and a miss on the line the market treats as reliable (2027 revenue consensus has moved 1% in twelve months while earnings fell by half). I hold this with less confidence than the rest: the second set's Core figure rests on quarterly splits it derived itself (accurate, on its own statement, to about SEK 20m a quarter) and on an inference about the base. The Consumer should present it as the first dated point at which our forecast and the brokers' part company, not as a prediction of a miss. If it happens, I expect Deutsche Bank to take 2027 EBITDA toward SEK 320m to 335m and earnings per share toward 5.0 to 5.3.
  2. Early May 2027 (Q1). Margin level with a year earlier by construction. The first quarter under the 2027 supplier contracts: the one place in the window where a positive surprise is plausible (D-02).
  3. Mid-August and early November 2027 (Q2, Q3). The margin step (5.3%, then 6.3%). If delivered, 2027 estimates settle near our figures and the argument moves to 2028, where the brokers are above us mainly on Growth revenue (the geographic consensus has Growth up 48% in 2027 and 37.5% in 2028, against our 34% and 30%).

Why I say the estimates have been reset less than they look, and where I disagree with the first set. The first set read the FactSet series (2027 earnings per share down 53% in twelve months, 20% in six, 5 to 7% in one) as estimates "still being cut month by month" and "cut deeply and broadly". With three contributors the mean mostly records who is in it. DNB Carnegie, with 7.00 for 2027, left on 26 June 2026. Pareto, with 3.58, is now in. A year ago the mean was 10.8, from brokers that no longer contribute. The median broker tells a different story: Deutsche Bank's 2027 figure was 5.26 in April 2026 (as cited from its note of 20 April in the research report on tax; I have not seen the note) and is 5.80 now, while its 2026 figure went from 3.14 to 2.19. It has not lowered the recovery; it has moved it into 2027 and steepened it, from growth of about 68% to about 165%. Over the last three months consensus 2027 EBITDA rose 6% and target prices rose 21%. The reset the first set described has happened to 2026 and at Pareto. At the median, 2027 and 2028 are untouched, which leaves more room to fall than a 53% decline suggests.

Why the brokers may hold or raise instead: Core beat Deutsche Bank's estimate by 15% in Q3 2026 and its 2027 Core margin (12.1%) is below the Q3 level; the 2027 supplier contracts may be strong while manufacturers fight for share; Deutsche Bank has already lowered its 2027 Growth margin to -4.5%, close to our -5.2%; and the conventions (tax, share count) cannot be disproved by a quarter.

Breadth and levels, my estimates. With three brokers, net breadth moves in steps of a third. After February: one down (Deutsche Bank), one flat or up (Pareto), one unknown; 2027 median down 5 to 12%, mean down 3 to 8%. By November 2027: 2027 median about 4.8 to 5.2; 2027 revenue consensus (SEK 5,802m) lower by 2 to 5% (about 60% likely); 2028 median (9.42) lower by 10 to 20% (about 65% likely). In months 12 to 18, if the margin step has been delivered, estimates stop falling; rising estimates need Growth revenue up about 40% with costs held, to which I give about 18%. The dashboard's rating for sell-side earnings momentum (D; revision score -2) would improve only then.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years: our forecast is 26% and about 31% below consensus for 2028 and 2029 (9.42; 14.00, the latter from one broker), mainly on Growth revenue, which the brokers take from management's target. That consensus is more likely cut than held (about 65 to 70%) over 2027 and 2028, unless United States online penetration accelerates, which is the one development that would move our own forecast up toward theirs.

D-0755
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

–55

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: almost wholly idiosyncratically. Over the latest six months the equal-weighted basket of the five tobacco peers explains 1% of the variance of Haypp's daily returns (correlation 0.12, beta 0.34); in none of the 24 month-end readings did it explain more than 7%, and in several the correlation was negative. The multiple is company-driven and, more precisely, driven by the company's own estimates: it is high because forecasts for 2028 have not yet been cut, not because the sector has re-rated. Two cautions: the basket contains manufacturers only, so the table does not test co-movement with Nordic online retailers or with small, illiquid growth shares in general; and thin trading (about USD 0.5m a day) pulls daily correlations down. For predictability this cuts both ways: there is no sector tide to forecast, and no sector support.

Evidence:

  • Price. Latest window (21 May to 13 November 2026): correlation with the basket 0.12, beta 0.34, 99% of variance the company's own. By peer: British American Tobacco 0.15, Scandinavian Tobacco 0.15, Imperial 0.09, Philip Morris Czech Republic -0.06, Karelia -0.09. Over 24 months the correlation ranged from -0.11 to 0.26; the two highest readings (0.26, in August and September 2025) cover the weeks in which ZYN returned to Haypp's United States sites, pouch news that moved manufacturers too. The six-month returns are similar (+6.9% against +5.8%) by coincidence of level, not of path.
  • Multiple. Haypp trades on 17.3 times 24-month-forward consensus earnings, the 83rd percentile of five years (range 6.8 to 35.0, median 13.2), and 19.3 times current earnings, the 87th percentile of ten years. The peers sit at unrelated points of their own histories: British American Tobacco at the 81st percentile (10.0 times), Imperial at the 35th (5.9 times), Scandinavian Tobacco at the 20th (7.0 times). There has been no common re-rating to explain Haypp's.
  • How the multiple got here. Over six months the consensus mean for 2027 earnings per share fell 20% while the price rose about 8%, so the multiple on 2027 earnings rose by about a third with the price barely moving; investors and brokers moved the anchor to 2028, whose consensus has fallen only 7%. On our earnings the two-year-forward multiple is 24.6 times, above the "mania" band (20.3 times, the 90th percentile of ten years). The multiple will therefore move with what happens to 2028 estimates, which is a company matter.
  • History. The five-year range of 6.8 to 35 times was travelled on the company's own events: the Swedish licence dispute, the ZYN pause and return, the 2026 profit shortfalls.

What follows for the Consumer: the share price can be anticipated only through the company's four reports a year and regulatory headlines; the dashboard's top rating for the tobacco theme tells nothing about the path of this share; a holding diversifies a tobacco or staples exposure almost completely; and if the multiple falls there is no peer anchor until manufacturer multiples of 6 to 10 times, which is why the first set's trough is so far below the price. The first set's warning that the present market regime sells small, illiquid growth shares is not tested by this table and stands.
