Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 15 November 2026, 10:51 to 11:22 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Haypp Group AB (HAYPP-SE): Third and fourth sets of questions: the predictability of the forecasts, and what guidance, sell-side estimates and the narrative already reflect

Written 2026-11-13 by the Investment Analyst for the Analyst who writes the investment memo.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Before the answers: what the Consumer needs to know first

Both sets in one paragraph. Haypp's forecast is predictable where it matters least for the shares and unpredictable where it matters most. Revenue and the direction of margins are predictable: demand is habitual, the mature segment funds everything, and the decisive segment is reported every quarter. The level and timing of earnings per share are not, for three reasons that reinforce one another. (1) The adjusted EBIT margin is 2.3%, so an error that is small against revenue is large against profit. (2) The whole forecast profit increase rests on one behaviour the company showed in 2024 and did not show in 2026: letting gross profit growth reach profit instead of spending it. (3) Almost all the gross profit growth comes from one income stream, Media and Insights, paid by the company's own suppliers. Against that, expectations sit above our forecast on every line: our revenue is 5% and 8% below the two brokers' in 2027 and 2028, our earnings per share about 27% below the brokers' median, our 2028 revenue about 9% below the company's target floor, and the published narrative is in substance one bullish broker's. On our earnings the shares trade on 24.6 times two-year-forward earnings against a guideline fair multiple of 14.45 times. The next two reports (10 February and early May 2027) can damage the thesis but cannot confirm it; the first that can confirm it is the second-quarter report in mid-August 2027.

Bases. "Our forecast" is the Financial Analyst's model (update 1), calculated from the second set's inputs: revenue SEK 4,750m (2026), 5,524m, 6,336m, 7,204m, 8,104m; adjusted EBITDA SEK 220m, 309m, 431m, 548m, 681m; earnings per share 1.89, 4.19, 6.97, 9.67, 12.80. Profit is on the company's adjusted basis (before items affecting comparability and before amortisation of acquired intangibles). "The two brokers" are Deutsche Bank and Pareto Securities, the only two giving revenue and EBITDA on FactSet; Barclays adds a third earnings-per-share figure. "My derivation" marks a figure I calculated from disclosed figures; the company does not publish it.

The gaps the model summary shows between the second set's cross-check and the model, explained.

  • Earnings per share (model 0.5% to 1.5% higher in every year: 1.89 against 1.88; 4.19 against 4.13; 6.97 against 6.87; 9.67 against 9.54; 12.80 against 12.62). Net income is identical; the difference is the share count. The model's net income divided by its earnings per share gives about 31.9m shares in 2027, 32.0m, 32.4m and 32.65m, about 0.45m fewer than the averages the second set used (32.4m, 32.5m, 32.8m, 33.1m). The model appears to carry only part of the 0.50m shares the second set added for 2026 (my inference; the summary does not show the count). The gap is immaterial, but checking it shows the 0.50m is a double count. The company's reported figures imply a basic share count of about 31.1m in Q3 2026 (net loss SEK 43.2m, loss per share 1.39) against about 30.6m in Q1 2026 (profit SEK 4.5m, 0.147 a share) and about 30.6m for 2025 (SEK 42.5m, 1.39). The 0.5m shares of the March 2026 warrant tender are therefore already in the company's count, and that count is below the 31.8m FactSet holds and the model starts from. I set the 2026 addition to zero.
  • Free cash flow, 2027 to 2030 (model higher by SEK 42m, 19m, 16m, 16m). This is the cash cost of items affecting comparability, for which the model has no line: SEK 30m plus payment of the USD 2.5m provision (about SEK 23.5m) in 2027, SEK 25m in 2028 and SEK 20m a year after, less tax relief at the input rate. No input is wrong. The second set's figures are the better guide to cash, and are if anything slightly high for 2027 (SEK 49m), because the provision sits in a loss-making United States company and will earn no tax relief; I would use about SEK 43m. On that basis 2027 free cash flow is under 1% of the market value (SEK 4.86bn) and the model's SEK 91.5m is about twice the likely figure.
  • Free cash flow, 2026 (model SEK 18m against SEK 43m). The second set built its figure from the actual nine-month operating cash flow (SEK 212.8m, including a SEK 112.8m working-capital inflow on the company's wide definition) less an assumed fourth-quarter reversal of about SEK 50m. The model applies working capital on FactSet's narrow definition (receivables plus inventories less payables), which rose by SEK 26m over the nine months (SEK 216m to SEK 242m) and which the 4.5% input brings back to about SEK 214m at year-end. Both are estimates of a quarter-end balance that has moved the company's net debt by SEK 90m to SEK 180m in each of the last three quarters (SEK -132m, -40m, -222m, -96m from December 2025 to September 2026). The gap is inside that noise; I leave the input.
  • The phasing note (2026 periods "moved by +3.0 points"). The model holds the three reported 2026 quarters on FactSet's "operating" EBITDA, which is after the cash part of items affecting comparability: SEK 125.4m for nine months against the company's adjusted SEK 162.8m. To reach the year's input (4.64% of revenue, SEK 220.4m, an adjusted figure) the model must put SEK 95m into Q4 2026, 7.6% of that quarter's revenue, where the second set intends 4.6% (SEK 57m). The year is right; the quarter is overstated by about SEK 37m. Because the model starts from actual net debt at 30 September 2026, that SEK 37m is added to year-end cash although it was spent in the first nine months. This, not trading, is the main reason the model's net debt falls from SEK 96m to SEK 33m in one quarter; in each of the last two years net debt rose in the fourth quarter (by SEK 19m in 2024 and SEK 30m in 2025). The Consumer should not quote the model's Q4 2026 margin or its net cash line. I judge net debt at end-2026 (leases included) more likely to be SEK 100m to 150m than SEK 33m, and the SEK 887m of net cash shown for 2030 too high by roughly SEK 150m to 200m once items affecting comparability (about SEK 90m after tax over 2027 to 2030) are also deducted. None of this touches earnings per share or the return calculation.

The inputs I change (file inputs-changed.md). Two lines; the reasons are in D-01.

  • Tax rate: 15%, 25%, 23%, 21%, 20% (was 28%, 25%, 24%, 23%, 22%). For 2026 the nine-month accounts show a tax charge of about SEK 3.7m on adjusted pre-tax profit of about SEK 60m, so 28% for the year cannot be reached. For 2029 and 2030 the second set's own forecast has the Growth segment in profit, at which point there are no unrelieved losses left to hold the rate above the Swedish 20.6%.
  • Change in the share count, 2026: 0.00 (was 0.50); later years unchanged.
  • Effect, approximately: 2026 earnings per share rises from 1.89 to about 2.25 (the brokers' median is 2.19 and their EBITDA is the same as ours, so the 14% shortfall to consensus that the model shows for 2026 was a tax-rate artefact, not a view); 2027 is almost unchanged at about 4.2; 2028 rises by about 2% and 2029 and 2030 by about 3 to 4%. The return at the guideline multiple moves from -3.0% to about zero at the 18-month column. The conclusions below do not depend on it.

Requests to the Financial Analyst. The Financial Analyst should add a line for the cash cost of items affecting comparability (SEK 53.5m in 2027, 25m in 2028, 20m a year in 2029 and 2030), so that free cash flow and net cash include it. The Financial Analyst should hold the three reported 2026 quarters on the company's adjusted EBITDA (SEK 107.3m for the first half and SEK 55.5m for Q3), or otherwise stop the 3.0-point shift, so that Q4 2026 shows the intended 4.6% margin and end-2026 cash is not raised by SEK 37m. The Financial Analyst should state which share count the model divides by, since FactSet's 31.8m is above the company's basic count of about 31.1m.

Seven findings new in this set, each worked through below.

  1. The forecast needs nothing new from gross profit, which has grown by SEK 150m to 195m a year for three years and is forecast to grow by SEK 167m to 198m. It needs operating costs to grow by SEK 78m in 2027 after SEK 211m in 2026 (D-01, D-02).
  2. Media and Insights by segment, my derivation: about SEK 106m in Core in Q3 2026 (up about 49%) and about SEK 42m in Growth (up about 26%, half the pace of Growth revenue). Core's record margin is supplier income, not volume leverage; and the United States customer base has not yet been priced into supplier contracts (D-02, D-03).
  3. A Growth customer already spends about as much as a Core customer (SEK 1,713 against SEK 1,829 a quarter). Growth loses money because half as many customers carry more overhead, not because the United States customer is worth less (D-02).
  4. The gross-margin input contains a cushion: it implies the product margin falling from 8.4% to 7.3% in 2027, when it was flat year on year in Q3 2026 (D-01).
  5. The two brokers imply Q4 2026 revenue of about SEK 1,295m (+23%); our forecast is SEK 1,248m (+18.6%). The first test, on 10 February 2027, falls on the one line with a clean record (I-02).
  6. The median broker has not cut 2027: Deutsche Bank's 2027 earnings per share is 5.80 against 5.26 in April 2026. The fall in the consensus mean is largely a change in which brokers are in it (I-02).
  7. The 18-month return depends on earnings for the year to about March 2030, not on the next 18 months' results (D-04).

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of HAYPP-SE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-13 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Haypp Group AB (HAYPP-SE), the Stockholm-listed online retailer of nicotine pouches and snus (storefronts including Snusbolaget, Nicokick, Northerner and Haypp), reporting two segments, Core Markets (Sweden and Norway) and Growth Markets (United States, United Kingdom, Germany, Switzerland and, from July 2026, Saudi Arabia), plus a Media and Insights business that sells retail media, launch services and consumer data to nicotine manufacturers

Request: OWN-1

Question: What makes up Haypp Group AB's (HAYPP-SE) operating costs between gross profit and adjusted EBITDA, and how has each part grown from 2023 to the third quarter of 2026: personnel costs, other external costs, marketing (3.2% of net sales in Q3 2026, company interim report, 6 November 2026), fulfilment (1.7% of net sales) and general and administrative costs (10.4% of net sales in Q3 2026 against 11.3% in Q2 2026, company; total overheads 15.2% of net sales, Deutsche Bank, 6 November 2026), together with full-time employees by function and by region (304 at Q3 2026 against 261 a year earlier, company) and capitalised work on own account (SEK 41.0 million in 2025 against SEK 32.4 million in 2024, annual report 2025)? Did the classification of costs between cost of goods sold and operating costs change between 2024 and 2025, when the company's reported gross margin rose from 15.0% for 2024 to 19.2% in Q2 2025 (company)? Which of these costs produce the Media and Insights revenue (analysts, the data platform, studies for manufacturers, the sensory panel due in 2027, Deutsche Bank, 6 November 2026), and what has management said about hiring, overhead growth and marketing for 2027, including in the months before the United Kingdom advertising and sponsorship ban of 1 June 2027? For comparison, how did overhead costs grow at Chewy, Zalando, Boozt, Apotea, Lyko, Redcare Pharmacy and Maplebear (Instacart) in the two years after each said that an organisational or infrastructure build-out was complete, how much additional overhead did their retail-media or advertising income require as it grew, and how often did management spend the resulting operating leverage instead of reporting it as profit?

Request: OWN-2

Question: What do Barclays (Overweight, target price SEK 210, FactSet, 6 November 2026) and Pareto Securities (Hold, target price SEK 160, FactSet, 9 November 2026) say in their research on Haypp Group AB (HAYPP-SE): the reasoning behind each rating, their forecasts for Growth Markets revenue and EBITDA margin, Media and Insights, the tax rate and items affecting comparability for 2026 to 2028, and what each expects from the Q4 2026 report on 10 February 2027? What do investors say about Haypp on X (Twitter), on Swedish investor forums and media (Placera, Avanza, Dagens industri, Borsvarlden, Affarsvarlden), in Nordic small-cap newsletters and in United States microcap commentary since the Q3 2026 report of 6 November 2026: which topics dominate, which are absent, and how is the slowing of reported growth as the 11 September 2025 return of ZYN to Haypp's United States sites is lapped (which Deutsche Bank expected to temper growth, 7 October 2026) being read? How has the shareholder register changed over the last twelve months (GR8 Ventures, Patrik Rees, Fidelity, Northerner Holding, Robotti and Company, and insiders including the chief executive), what short interest and stock lending exist, and which Nordic institutions exclude the shares as tobacco-related? State near the top which covering houses and which investor sources were actually found and used, and which were not.

Request: OWN-3

Question: How have forward price-to-earnings and EV/EBITDA multiples and share prices behaved, over the 6 to 18 months either side of the low point of an investment phase, at listed online specialists whose earnings estimates were being cut while brokers' target prices rose: Chewy, Maplebear (Instacart), Zalando, Boozt, Apotea, Lyko, BHG Group and Redcare Pharmacy? For each: at what multiple the shares traded at the margin low, how far the multiple moved before the first quarter of confirmed margin recovery and after it, how long that took, and what distinguished the cases that re-rated from those that de-rated. What did Haypp Group AB's (HAYPP-SE) own forward multiple and share price do, with dates, around the Swedish licence dispute (2024), the pause and return of ZYN in the United States (mid-2024 to 11 September 2025), the first-quarter 2026 margin shortfall (report of 7 May 2026) and the Q3 2026 report (6 November 2026), given a 24-month-forward price-to-earnings multiple of 17.3 times, at the 83rd percentile of its five-year range of 6.8 to 35.0 times (FactSet, 6 November 2026)? Which valuation methods and multiples do brokers use for Haypp, and why (Deutsche Bank target price SEK 220, Barclays SEK 210, Pareto Securities SEK 160, FactSet, November 2026; DNB Carnegie's last published scenario values before it ended coverage on 26 June 2026)?
